### Relationships used in WAL data

- **Scoped into assessments** via `assessment_scope_assets` — e.g. ASM-019 (`f3ee1dc9…`) included AST-019; ASM-010 (`c4f2a710…`) included AST-001, AST-003, AST-004.
- **Linked to threats / vulnerabilities / cyber risks** via the `asset_threats`, `asset_vulnerability_categories`, `asset_cyber_risks` join tables (populated by `seed-relationships.ts`).
- **Linked to controls** via `control_assets`.

**Domain distribution** — Technology (26), Process (3), People (1).
**Vulnerability type families** — Patch/Update Management (5), Authentication & Access Control (4), Network Security Weakness (4), Cryptographic Weakness (3), Logging/Monitoring Gap (3), Identity & Privilege Mgmt (2), Data Protection (2), Security Configuration (2), plus singletons for App Security Defect, Cloud Security Misconfiguration, Policy & Governance, Security Awareness, Third-Party Risk, Incident Response Readiness.

### Relationships

- `asset_vulnerability_categories` (asset_id ↔ vulnerability_category_id) — populated by `seed-relationships.ts`.
- `scenario_vulnerability_categories` (scenario_id ↔ vulnerability_category_id) — written when a scenario is built around a given vulnerability.
- Scenarios also store a denormalized `vulnerability_id` field directly (single-link convenience).
- `vulnerability_category_cia_impacts` — child rows enumerated above in the CIA impacts column.

---

**Status distribution** — Monitoring (4), Mitigation (4), Assessment (3), Identification (3), Draft (1).
**Domain coverage** — 10 unique domains, with Data & Information / Network & Infrastructure / Endpoint & Device / Application & API each appearing twice (the cross-cutting risks).

**Note on the WAL**: scenarios in the recovered data use `CR-001`, `CR-002`, `CR-005` (e.g. ASM-010 scenarios SC-001…SC-007). The `primaryAssetTypes` field is *informational only* — actual asset↔risk linkage lives in `asset_cyber_risks`, populated by `seed-relationships.ts`.

### Relationships

- `asset_cyber_risks` (asset_id ↔ cyber_risk_id) — populated by `seed-relationships.ts`.
- `control_cyber_risks` (control_id ↔ cyber_risk_id) — controls that mitigate the risk.
- `scenarios.cyber_risk_id` — denormalized FK on each scenario (a scenario is always built around one cyber risk).

---

**Type** — Preventive (27), Detective (8).
**Key controls** — 21 of 35 (Yes).
**Effectiveness** — 5/Very high (9), 4/High (20), 3/Medium (6); none below 3.
**Frequency** — Continuous (22), Weekly (5), Quarterly (4), Annually (3), Monthly (3), Bi-weekly (1).
**Highest coverage** — CR-005 (Sensitive data exfiltration) is mitigated by 8 controls; CR-001 by 5; CR-002 by 6.

### Relationships

- `control_cyber_risks` — many-to-many between controls and cyber risks. Shown flattened above; the seed also writes asset-type-based asset links via `control_assets`.
- `control_assets` (control_id ↔ asset_id) — populated by the seed by matching `assetTypes` keys (`software`, `hardware`, `cloud`, `information system`) to assets of the equivalent `asset_type`.
- Controls feed the **residual scoring** path: when `cyber_risk_assessments.scoring_type = 'residual'`, scenarios fold in the mitigating controls' effectiveness.

---

## 7. Scenarios (`scenarios` + `scenario_relationships`)

271 unique scenarios recovered from the WAL, spread across 13 of the 28 assessments (the other 15 had not yet generated scenarios). ASM-027 and ASM-028 reached `ai_scoring_phase = complete` per their parent assessment rows; the underlying scenario score columns may exist in the WAL but were not recoverable via the strings-based extraction.

Each row is one (assessment × asset × cyber risk × threat) tuple. Scenarios are typically generated by Cartesian product over the assessment's scoped assets, asset-linked cyber risks, and asset-linked threats; vulnerability_id is set when an asset has a primary linked vulnerability category.

**Count per assessment**: ASM-010 (7), ASM-015 (18), ASM-018 (18), ASM-019 (18), ASM-020 (30), ASM-021 (30), ASM-022 (36), ASM-023 (36), ASM-024 (24), ASM-025 (18), ASM-026 (12), ASM-027 (12), ASM-028 (12).

**Asset coverage** — only 7 distinct assets in scope across all recovered scenarios: AST-001 Customer Database Server, AST-002 Active Directory Service, AST-003 ERP System, AST-004 Corporate VPN Gateway, AST-019 Access Control System, AST-028 API Gateway, AST-055 Antivirus Management, AST-102 Benefits Administration, AST-137 Audit Evidence Vault.

### Relationships

- `scenarios.assessment_id` → `cyber_risk_assessments.id` (ON DELETE CASCADE).
- `scenarios.asset_id`, `cyber_risk_id`, `threat_id`, `vulnerability_id` — denormalized display-ID references (no FK constraint; soft links).
- `scenario_relationships` (scenario_id ↔ entity_type/entity_id) — additional links to threats / vulnerabilities / controls beyond the primary FK fields; not yet populated in the WAL data.
- Legacy join tables `scenario_threats` and `scenario_vulnerability_categories` exist from migrate.ts but were superseded by the denormalized columns in the newer schema (`scenarios_old` backup may still hold those rows).

### What's missing

The recovered scenario rows contain only structural data (assessment, asset, cyber risk, threat, vulnerability). Score columns (`impact`, `threat_severity`, `vulnerability_severity`, `likelihood`, `cyber_risk_score`, `scoring_rationale`, `scored_at`, `scored_by`) weren't recoverable via the strings extraction. ASM-027 and ASM-028 are flagged `complete` at the assessment level, so their scenarios likely *do* have scores stored — but those numeric fields don't surface in plain text strings of the WAL.
